When upgrading to a new major or minor version, it's important to check for any breaking changes, especially those affecting TypeScript types.\\n\\n### **Emittery 0.8.0 and TypeScript Typed Breaking Changes**\\n\\nBased on the [Emittery changelog](https://github.com/sindresorhus/emittery/releases) and discussions/issues on their GitHub repo, here is an overview:\\n\\n#### **Key Breaking Changes (TypeScript-Typed Specific):**\\n\\n1. **Type Changes for `on` and `emit`**\\n   - Prior to v0.8.0, listeners and emitters were sometimes typed as `any`. Starting from v0.8.0, typing for event names and payloads became stricter.\\n   - If you have custom event objects, you'll want to update your emitter definition:\\n     ```ts\\n     interface Events {\\n       greeting: string;\\n       data: { id: number }\\n     }\\n     const emitter = new Emittery<Events>();\\n     ```\\n   - Using string literals for events is required for type safety.\\n\\n2. **All APIs Now Fully Typed**\\n   - You **must** supply a type/mapping for your events if you want strong typing. If you don't, event payloads are now typed as `unknown`, which forces you to use type assertions.\\n\\n3. **Removed/Changed Methods**\\n   - The type for returned values from `emit`, `on`, `off`, etc., may have stricter variance, breaking old code if you relied on looser types.\\n\\n#### **Migration Concerns:**\\n\\n- **TypeScript Errors on Event Payloads:**  \\n  If you previously used plain strings for events and passed any payload (untyped), you'll now get type errors unless you define an event map interface.\\n- **Type Inference:**  \\n  Calling `emitter.on('event', handler)` now requires the handler to conform to the event's mapped type, or `unknown` if none is supplied.\\n\\n#### **Example:**\\n\\n_Pre-0.8.0 (looser typing):_\\n```ts\\nconst emitter = new Emittery();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) => { /* payload: any */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\n_0.8.0+ (strict typing):_\\n```ts\\ninterface Events {\\n  hello: { world: boolean };\\n}\\nconst emitter = new Emittery<Events>();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) => { /* payload: { world: boolean } */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\nIf you **do not** provide a generic, payloads will be `unknown`, and TypeScript will complain if you use payloads unsafely.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **What to Do When Migrating:**\\n\\n- **Define an Event Mapping Interface:**  \\n  Whenever you construct a new `Emittery` instance, provide a mapping of event names to their payload types.\\n- **Check Handler Signatures:**  \\n  Update your handler function arguments to match the new types.\\n- **Update Tests/Mocks:**  \\n  If you use mocks, ensure they use correct type signatures.\\n\\n---\\n\\n**References:**\\n- [Emittery v0.8.0 is a popular TypeScript-friendly event emitter library. When upgrading to a new major or minor version, it's important to check for any breaking changes, especially those affecting TypeScript types.\\n\\n### **Emittery 0.8.0 and TypeScript Typed Breaking Changes**\\n\\nBased on the [Emittery changelog](https://github.com/sindresorhus/emittery/releases) and discussions/issues on their GitHub repo, here is an overview:\\n\\n#### **Key Breaking Changes (TypeScript-Typed Specific):**\\n\\n1. **Type Changes for `on` and `emit`**\\n   - Prior to v0.8.0, listeners and emitters were sometimes typed as `any`. Starting from v0.8.0, typing for event names and payloads became stricter.\\n   - If you have custom event objects, you'll want to update your emitter definition:\\n     ```ts\\n     interface Events {\\n       greeting: string;\\n       data: { id: number }\\n     }\\n     const emitter = new Emittery<Events>();\\n     ```\\n   - Using string literals for events is required for type safety.\\n\\n2. **All APIs Now Fully Typed**\\n   - You **must** supply a type/mapping for your events if you want strong typing. If you don't, event payloads are now typed as `unknown`, which forces you to use type assertions.\\n\\n3. **Removed/Changed Methods**\\n   - The type for returned values from `emit`, `on`, `off`, etc., may have stricter variance, breaking old code if you relied on looser types.\\n\\n#### **Migration Concerns:**\\n\\n- **TypeScript Errors on Event Payloads:**  \\n  If you previously used plain strings for events and passed any payload (untyped), you'll now get type errors unless you define an event map interface.\\n- **Type Inference:**  \\n  Calling `emitter.on('event', handler)` now requires the handler to conform to the event's mapped type, or `unknown` if none is supplied.\\n\\n#### **Example:**\\n\\n_Pre-0.8.0 (looser typing):_\\n```ts\\nconst emitter = new Emittery();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) => { /* payload: any */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\n_0.8.0+ (strict typing):_\\n```ts\\ninterface Events {\\n  hello: { world: boolean };\\n}\\nconst emitter = new Emittery<Events>();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) => { /* payload: { world: boolean } */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\nIf you **do not** provide a generic, payloads will be `unknown`, and TypeScript will complain if you use payloads unsafely.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **What to Do When Migrating:**\\n\\n- **Define an Event Mapping Interface:**  \\n  Whenever you construct a new `Emittery` instance, provide a mapping of event names to their payload types.\\n- **Check Handler Signatures:**  \\n  Update your handler function arguments to match the new types.\\n- **Update Tests/Mocks:**  \\n  If you use mocks, ensure they use correct type signatures.\\n\\n---\\n\\n**References:**\\n- [Emittery v0.8.0 changelog](https://github.com/sindresorhus/emittery/releases/tag/v0.8.0)\\n- [Example migration issues](https://github.com/sindresorhus/emittery/issues/62)\\n- [Official Emittery Starting from v0.8.0, typing for event names and payloads became stricter.\\n   - If you have custom event objects, you'll want to update your emitter definition:\\n     ```ts\\n     interface Events {\\n       greeting: string;\\n       data: { id: number }\\n     }\\n     const emitter = new Emittery\\u003cEvents\\u003e();\\n     ```\\n   - Using string literals for events is required for type safety.\\n\\n2. **All APIs Now Fully Typed**\\n   - You **must** supply a type/mapping for your events if you want strong typing. If you don't, event payloads are now typed as `unknown`, which forces you to use type assertions.\\n\\n3. **Removed/Changed Methods**\\n   - The type for returned values from `emit`, `on`, `off`, etc., may have stricter variance, breaking old code if you relied on looser types.\\n\\n#### **Migration Concerns:**\\n\\n- **TypeScript Errors on Event Payloads:**  \\n  If you previously used plain strings for events and passed any payload (untyped), you'll now get type errors unless you define an event map interface.\\n- **Type Inference:**  \\n  Calling `emitter.on('event', handler)` now requires the handler to conform to the event's mapped type, or `unknown` if none is supplied.\\n\\n#### **Example:**\\n\\n_Pre-0.8.0 (looser typing):_\\n```ts\\nconst emitter = new Emittery();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) =\\u003e { /* payload: any */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\n_0.8.0+ (strict typing):_\\n```ts\\ninterface Events {\\n  hello: { world: boolean };\\n}\\nconst emitter = new Emittery\\u003cEvents\\u003e();\\nemitter.on('hello', (payload) =\\u003e { /* payload: { world: boolean } */ });\\nemitter.emit('hello', { world: true });\\n```\\n\\nIf you **do not** provide a generic, payloads will be `unknown`, and TypeScript will complain if you use payloads unsafely.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **What to Do When Migrating:**\\n\\n- **Define an Event Mapping Interface:**  \\n  Whenever you construct a new `Emittery` instance, provide a mapping of event names to their payload types.\\n- **Check Handler Signatures:**  \\n  Update your handler function arguments to match the new types.\\n- **Update Tests/Mocks:**  \\n  If you use mocks, ensure they use correct type signatures.\\n\\n---\\n\\n**References:**\\n- [Emittery v0.8.0 Event listeners can be registered with `on` or `once`, and events can be emitted with `emit`.\n \n `Emittery` has a generic `EventData` type that can be provided by users to strongly type the list of events and the data passed to the listeners for those events.
